## Article copy starts below

# Volunteering at NIAT’s Gesture-Control Workshop: ESP32, LEDs and Chaos

By K goutham sai

Controlling something with a hand gesture sounded like science fiction until our workshop at NIAT. As Gen AI Club President—and part-time chaos manager 😄—I volunteered for a session led by Vamsi sir that mixed learning with plenty of “why is this not working?” moments.

## A Demo, Then Wires Everywhere

The idea sounded simple: help students control systems using gestures. Vamsi sir’s demonstration got everyone’s attention. Then came the hands-on work: laptops, connections and a familiar question every few minutes—“Sir, why is my LED not glowing?”

“Bro, check my connection” and “I think my ESP32 is judging me” captured the mood pretty well. As volunteers, we moved around checking circuits, fixing loose wires and explaining the same steps again. Watching a student finally get a response made it worthwhile.

## What We Used and Built

The workshop used:

- ESP32 microcontrollers
- Sensors to detect motion
- Arduino IDE for coding

The basic sequence was straightforward: a sensor captured movement, data reached the system, code interpreted the gesture and an output responded. An LED turning on or off was one of those visible results. Your hand had become part of the control.

## The Moments That Made Us Laugh

One student celebrated a working LED as if he had cracked GSoC. Another connected wires in a way that seemed wrong to us, yet somehow got a response. Meanwhile, Vamsi sir calmly fixed in seconds what had kept us busy for twenty minutes.

Those moments made the technical work feel approachable, even when it was frustrating.

## What Volunteering Taught Me

I was learning alongside the students. Helping meant managing people and problems at the same time, staying calm and finding simpler ways to explain technical ideas.

My most persistent troubleshooting reminder from the day: check the GND connection early.

## Leaving With More Than a Working Circuit

For me, the workshop made gesture control feel like something we could explore in a classroom. The memory is a mixture of teamwork, working LEDs, loose wires and laughter. And next time, we are bringing extra kits. Definitely.

## Frequently Asked Questions

### What tools did we use in the workshop?

The account names ESP32 microcontrollers, motion sensors and Arduino IDE for coding.

### What did the gesture-control system do?

Sensors captured movement, code interpreted the data and an output responded, such as an LED turning on or off.

### What did volunteering teach me?

I practised explaining technical ideas, checking connections and staying calm while helping several students troubleshoot.

### Who led the gesture-control workshop?

Vamsi sir led the session. I helped as a volunteer while serving as Gen AI Club President.

### What troubleshooting problems did volunteers help with?

We checked circuits, fixed loose wires and helped students understand why their setup was not responding. Checking the GND connection became a memorable reminder from the day.

## Article copy starts below

# Accountability for Students: Taking Ownership of Goals and Mistakes

By K goutham sai

Accountability means taking ownership of your actions, choices and mistakes. For students, that can be as practical as completing an agreed task, acknowledging a missed deadline or asking what needs to change after a difficult assignment.

## Taking Ownership Without Getting Stuck in Blame

When something goes wrong, responsibility begins with looking honestly at your part in it. Asking “What can I do better next time?” gives you something to work on.

That does not make every problem entirely your fault. It means identifying the choices and commitments you can act on rather than stopping at an excuse.

## Why Accountability Matters in a Team

### Following Through Builds Trust

Classmates need to know whether they can rely on you. Doing what you promised—and speaking up when you cannot—helps a team plan its work. Admitting a mistake early also gives others a chance to help address it.

### Leadership Begins With Responsibility

You do not need a formal title to demonstrate leadership. Taking a task seriously and being honest about progress can encourage the same behaviour around you.

### Acknowledging Mistakes Makes Improvement Possible

It is difficult to change an approach without first recognizing what went wrong. Owning a mistake creates room for feedback, adjustment and another attempt.

## Three Ways to Practise Accountability

1. **Set clear goals.** Write down what you want to complete so the commitment is specific.
2. **Admit mistakes promptly.** Explain what happened and how you plan to address it.
3. **Ask for honest feedback.** Teachers and friends may notice gaps you have missed.

## Making Responsibility a Habit

Accountability develops through repeated choices. Setting a goal, following through and learning from mistakes can help students become more dependable. The useful question is what you will take responsibility for next.

## Frequently Asked Questions

### What does accountability mean for a student?

It means taking responsibility for your choices, following through on commitments and acknowledging what you can improve when something goes wrong.

### How can students practise accountability?

Set clear goals, admit mistakes promptly, explain how you will address them and ask teachers or friends for honest feedback.

### Why does accountability matter in a student team?

Following through on agreed work helps classmates rely on you. Being honest about a problem also gives the team a chance to address it.

### Can students show leadership without a formal title?

The article argues that taking responsibility, being honest and setting an example are ways to practise leadership before receiving a title.

### Why acknowledge a mistake before trying to improve?

Recognizing what went wrong makes it possible to identify an adjustment and use feedback. Avoiding the mistake makes that learning harder.

# Personal Growth at NIAT: What Responsibility and Setbacks Taught Me

By Vishali.k

During my first year at NIAT, I began to notice changes that were not measured by grades. I was learning how to take responsibility, respond to setbacks and become more comfortable trying something unfamiliar.

Personal growth happened gradually, through experiences rather than one dramatic turning point.

## Taking Responsibility Before Feeling Ready

Presentations, competitions, projects and leadership roles could feel intimidating. Taking part taught me that confidence often develops after you act.

Responsibility also changed how I approached work. When other people depend on a task, deadlines and commitments matter in a different way. I began connecting discipline and accountability with everyday participation.

## Learning With Other People

The Gen AI Club gave students opportunities to organize events, work on projects, collaborate and explore ideas. Those activities involved personal development alongside technical learning.

Meeting people with different backgrounds and perspectives mattered too. Some of the lessons that stayed with me came through conversations. Listening helped me see beyond my own assumptions.

## What the BRAVE Competition Taught Me

My account of the BRAVE Competition includes nearly 15 unsuccessful business negotiations before a successful 16th attempt. The setbacks were disappointing, but they gave me things to work on.

Communication, confidence and resilience improved through that process. Looking back, I connect the eventual success with what the earlier attempts taught me.

## Turning Experience Into Learning

I learned to reflect on what had worked, what needed improvement and what I could carry into the next situation. Experience became more useful when I took time to examine it.

Leadership also started to feel less dependent on a title. Helping someone, taking initiative or contributing to a solution could be an opportunity to practise it.

Curiosity kept that process going. Exploring ideas beyond the syllabus and accepting new responsibilities gave me more chances to learn.

## Recognizing the Change

I did not always notice progress as it happened. It became clearer when I compared myself with how I had felt months earlier. For me, one of the valuable parts of college has been recognizing that growth: greater confidence, a better response to setbacks and more willingness to participate.

Taking responsibility, participating in presentations and projects, and reflecting on setbacks helped me become more willing to act.

## Article copy starts below

# Realistic Optimism for Students: Facing Challenges and Finding a Next Step

By K goutham sai

Difficult exams, demanding projects and disappointing results are part of many students’ experience. Realistic optimism means acknowledging those difficulties while looking for something constructive to do next.

It does not require pretending everything is fine.

## Looking for a Way Forward

A positive outlook can help direct attention toward possible responses. If a study plan is not working, the useful question is what needs to change: the approach, the amount of practice or the support you seek.

Recognizing a problem and believing improvement is possible can exist together.

## Learning From a Setback

Failure can be discouraging. Giving yourself room to acknowledge that disappointment does not prevent you from learning from it.

Asking “What can this mistake teach me?” shifts the focus toward information you can use. A difficult project might reveal a gap in understanding; an unsuccessful plan might show where expectations were unrealistic.

## Practising Realistic Optimism

The article’s central idea is to combine hope with an honest view of the situation:

- Recognize what is difficult rather than dismissing it.
- Consider another approach when the current one is not helping.
- Ask for support and keep learning from feedback.

Believing you can improve is different from guaranteeing a particular result. A hard subject may still require time, practice and help.

## Carrying the Mindset Into College Life

Optimism is useful when it supports action. Facing a challenge clearly, identifying a possible next step and remaining willing to learn can make a difficult period more manageable. The aim is a constructive response, even when the outcome is uncertain.

## Frequently Asked Questions

### Does optimism mean ignoring a difficult situation?

No. The article argues for recognizing a problem honestly while looking for a practical next step.

### What might that look like during college?

If a study plan is not working, review the approach, ask for help and use mistakes to identify what needs more practice.

### How can a student learn from an unsuccessful attempt?

The article suggests looking at what the attempt reveals, such as a gap in understanding or an approach that needs to change, rather than treating the result as the end of learning.

### Does a positive outlook guarantee a good exam result?

No. A constructive mindset can support action, but a difficult subject still requires learning, practice and sometimes help.

### Can students acknowledge disappointment and still be optimistic?

Yes. Realistic optimism makes room for an honest response to difficulty while remaining open to a useful next step.

## Article copy starts below

# Organizing NIAT NSRIT Sports Day: Cricket, Kabaddi, Volleyball and Dodgeball

By Dikshit Kumar

I'm the President of the Sports Club at NIAT at NSRIT, and we organized something I'm actually proud of—a full inter-campus sports collaboration with NSRIT. Cricket, kabaddi, volleyball, dodgeball. One day. Students from NIAT and NSRIT. Real competition, real energy.

## Why We Started This

Sports at college is not just about staying fit. It's about building a culture where you actually feel part of something bigger than your course. We realized that NIAT students were either grinding in labs or sitting in classrooms, and nobody was thinking about what competition across campuses could look like.

So we reached out to NSRIT. The idea was simple: let's build something that forces students to think about teamwork, discipline, and actually showing up for each other on the field. Not just attending class. Actual collaboration.

## What Happened on Game Day

We structured everything. Cricket was early morning—by 9 AM the first match was already intense. People who've never played competitively were out there. Kabaddi came next. Volleyball after lunch. Dodgeball as the finale when everyone was already tired but nobody wanted to leave.

The thing that got to me was the energy. Not just from players, but from people on the sidelines. Faculty came. They weren't just supervising—they were genuinely invested. First-year students were watching fourth-years play and learning what happens when you actually commit to something physical.

This wasn't just another college event. It was proof that when you give students a real platform, they don't need motivation. They bring it themselves.

## What Made It Work

- Fair rules and structured scheduling—everyone knew when their match was happening
- Faculty support that actually mattered—they didn't just sign off, they participated
- Student volunteers from both campuses who cared enough to show up early and stay late
- Real competition—we weren't handing out participation trophies, we had actual winners

The coordination was messy. There were moments at 2 AM when I was still planning schedules and wondering if anyone would actually show up. But the team executed. And that's what surprised me most—when you give students responsibility, they rise to it.

## What This Means

Sports events like these aren't about winning. They're about stress relief, confidence, leadership, and cooperation. Every student who played learned something. Every student who organized learned something different. Every student who watched learned that their campus is connected to another one.

The inter-campus connection matters. NIAT and NSRIT students aren't isolated. When they compete fairly and respect each other after the game, that builds something real. That's the culture we want to build.

Afterwards, we began thinking about another event, with stronger organization and the possibility of involving more campuses. For me, that ambition came from seeing what the volunteers, players and faculty had already made possible together.

## Frequently Asked Questions

### Which sports were part of the event?

Cricket began in the morning, followed by kabaddi, volleyball after lunch and dodgeball at the end.

### What helped the event run?

Clear rules, a structured schedule, faculty involvement and student volunteers from NIAT and NSRIT helped the team manage the day.

### What was my role in organizing the sports collaboration?

As Sports Club President, I helped organize the event and coordinate its schedule. I remember still working on plans at 2 AM.

### How did spectators and faculty contribute to the atmosphere?

The energy came from people on the sidelines as well as players. Faculty were involved, and students from different years watched and participated.

### What did I take away from organizing the event?

Seeing students take responsibility made me proud of the team. I valued the cooperation between NIAT and NSRIT alongside what happened in the matches.

# Learning AI at NIAT: Practical Opportunities and Beginner Challenges

By Santosh Reddy

Learning AI at NIAT brings opportunities to explore practical applications, alongside the challenge of understanding a complex and changing subject. This student-written overview considers both sides without treating learning as an automatic route to a job.

## Opportunities to Apply AI Concepts

Projects, workshops, technical events and collaborative learning can help connect theory with practical problems. AI also offers opportunities to explore automation, data analysis and decision-making.

Possible project areas include chatbots, recommendation systems, computer vision and intelligent robots. These are examples of applications students can investigate, rather than completed projects documented in this account.

AI connects programming, mathematics, data analysis, electronics and other disciplines. That breadth creates reasons for students with different interests to work together.

## Challenges When Starting Out

### Understanding Unfamiliar Concepts

Machine learning, neural networks, algorithms and data science can feel overwhelming initially. Learning requires patience and a willingness to revisit ideas that do not make sense straight away.

### Keeping Up With Changing Tools

New tools and approaches make ongoing learning part of the process. Curiosity and disciplined practice matter beyond completing a particular classroom topic.

### Working With Available Resources

Projects can raise questions about datasets, computing resources and technical guidance. Using available resources thoughtfully and seeking support from mentors, faculty or learning communities are part of the challenge.

## Learning to Use AI Responsibly

Building a working system is only part of the task. Privacy, fairness, transparency and responsible use also deserve attention. Students need room to question how an application works and how it may affect people.

## A Learning Process That Continues

The opportunity lies in developing technical understanding and problem-solving through sustained work. The challenges are part of that process. Exploring applications, asking for guidance and reflecting on responsible use can make AI learning more meaningful than simply following the excitement around a new technology.

## Frequently Asked Questions

### What makes AI challenging for beginners?

The article identifies unfamiliar concepts, changing tools, access to data and computing resources, and the need for ongoing practice and guidance.

### What kinds of projects does it discuss?

Chatbots, recommendation systems, computer vision and intelligent robots appear as examples of possible applications, not documented student builds.

### Why does responsible AI use matter here?

The article asks students to consider privacy, fairness and transparency alongside technical development.

### Why does the article connect AI with different disciplines?

It describes connections among computer science, mathematics, data analysis, electronics and other fields, creating opportunities for students with different interests to collaborate.

### Where can students seek help with AI project difficulties?

The article points to mentors, faculty members and learning communities as sources of guidance when concepts, data or computing resources create challenges.

# Shades of Serenity: A NIAT Media Club Color Collage

By K goutham sai

The Media Club’s Color Collage Competition offered a way to look more closely at everyday surroundings. In the collage described here, sky, campus architecture, greenery and sunlight come together in a quiet visual story.

## Blue Skies and Warm Light

The dominant blues suggest peace, openness and imagination to me. White clouds and golden sunlight add warmth. Some frames contain dramatic skies; others focus on brighter daylight or calmer landscapes.

Those differences give each photograph its own mood while allowing the images to sit together as a composition.

## Nature Alongside Campus Architecture

Clouds spreading across the sky and sunlight reaching through trees draw attention to natural patterns. Campus buildings and other structures appear alongside them, bringing human-made forms into the same view.

The interest lies partly in observation: noticing light and colour in scenes that might otherwise pass without a second look.

## Telling a Story Across Several Frames

The collage does not depend on a single subject. Its arrangement connects different perspectives, creating a sense of continuity and rhythm. I read the overall mood as one of tranquillity, freedom and inspiration.

## What the Competition Encouraged

The event encouraged participants to experiment with composition and communicate through images. This collage captures that idea for me: familiar surroundings can become expressive when someone pays attention to how colours, light and subjects work together.

## Frequently Asked Questions

### What does this collage bring together?

The author describes sky, clouds, sunlight, greenery and campus architecture arranged across multiple photographs.

### How does the author interpret its colours?

Blue suggests calm and openness, while warm light adds hope. These are the author’s artistic interpretations, not fixed meanings of colour.

The author describes different perspectives coming together with continuity and rhythm, rather than relying on one subject to carry the entire mood.

### What role does campus architecture play in the collage?

Buildings and structures appear alongside greenery, sky and sunlight, bringing human-made forms and natural scenery into the same composition.

### What did the competition encourage students to practise?

It encouraged closer observation, experimentation with composition and expressing a mood through images of familiar surroundings.

# Kailasagiri at Sunset: My Favourite Evening View in Vizag

By K goutham sai

One of my favourite evening experiences in Vizag has been watching the sky change from Kailasagiri. The hilltop view brings the city and coastline into the same scene, and it gave me a welcome pause from classes, assignments and projects.

## Watching the Evening Arrive

During my visit, the sunlight softened and the sky shifted through orange, pink and gold. City lights began appearing below. The atmosphere felt slower, and I found myself paying attention to details I would usually miss during a busy day.

I noticed other visitors pause too. Conversations became quieter, and people who had been taking photographs turned their attention toward the view.

## The Coastline, Sea and City Lights

From Kailasagiri, I could look along the coastline, see evening light on the Bay of Bengal and watch the city gradually light up. That combination is what stays with me.

Different evenings had different colours. Some were vivid; others were softer. The briefness of the changing light made me want to appreciate it while it lasted.

## Making Time to Pause

College life can keep attention fixed on the next task. Standing above the city gave me a different sense of perspective.

The BRAVE Competition later reminded me of a related lesson: while working toward a goal, it helps to recognize how far you have come. For me, an evening view offered space for that kind of reflection.

## Why This View Became a Favourite

I remember the feeling as much as the scenery: calm, reflective and present. I may forget individual evenings, but the colours, coastline and emerging city lights remain clear. That is why Kailasagiri became one of my favourite places to pause in Vizag.

## Frequently Asked Questions

### What made Kailasagiri memorable for me?

I remember the changing sky, views of the coastline and the city lights appearing below as evening approached.

### Why did the evening view feel like a break from college?

Watching the changing light gave me a chance to slow down and pay attention to the moment rather than the next assignment or project.

### What changes did I notice as evening approached?

The sunlight softened, the sky changed colour and city lights began to appear. I also noticed conversations becoming quieter as people watched the view.

### Did every evening view feel the same?

No. I describe some evenings as vivid and others as softer, with the changing colours making each experience feel different.

### How did the view connect with my reflections on achievement?

It gave me space to appreciate the present and how far I had come, an idea I later connected with the BRAVE Competition.

## Article copy starts below

# Teaching at ZPH High School, Andhapuram: My Day as “Anna”

By K goutham sai

I went to ZPH High School, Andhapuram thinking I would take a session, teach something useful and come back. Simple plan. Yeah… that did not quite happen 😄

## “Don’t Call Me Sir”

When I entered, the students stood up and said, “Good morning sir!” My immediate response was, “Don’t call me sir… call me anna or bro.”

That changed the atmosphere. They relaxed, I relaxed, and the room began to feel more like a conversation than a formal lesson.

Then came the age question. Without thinking, I said I was 30. My actual age was 18. Some believed me; others clearly did not. One student looked as though he was running a background check.

## Questions From More Than 100 Students

The group included students from classes 7 and 9—more than 100 in total. I had expected some hesitation, but every question drew an answer. Sometimes it drew everyone’s answer at once.

I kept asking questions, letting them guess and allowing interruptions. The session became a back-and-forth of reactions, answers and laughter, with learning happening between them. Managing that much energy was part of the experience.

## The Suno Demonstration I Will Remember

I decided to demonstrate generating a song with AI using Suno. In my head, it was going to be something cool.

What appeared was a full love song.

For a moment, the class went quiet. Then everyone started laughing, teasing friends and pointing across the room. I stood there thinking, “Nice… great decision, Goutham.”

## What the Students Taught Me

Their interest stood out. They were asking, reacting and answering rather than waiting for the session to end. Talking naturally and inviting them into the discussion worked better for me than trying to deliver a formal lecture.

## Would I Go Back?

Absolutely. I left thinking about their questions and the energy in the room as much as what I had taught. Next time, though, I am choosing the song topic very carefully.

## Frequently Asked Questions

### Who took part in the school session?

My account describes more than 100 students from classes 7 and 9 at ZPH High School, Andhapuram.

### What happened during the AI demonstration?

I used Suno to generate a song, but it produced a love song. The unexpected result sent the class into laughter.

### What did the visit teach me?

Speaking naturally, asking questions and allowing students to respond made the session feel more like a conversation.

### Why did I ask students to call me “anna” or “bro”?

After their greeting of “Good morning sir,” I asked for a more informal name. That helped both the students and me relax into the conversation.

### What happened when students asked my age?

I jokingly said I was 30, although I was actually 18. Some believed it and others were clearly unconvinced, which became one of the day’s funny moments.

# When Parents Ask Fewer Questions: A Student’s Reflection on Their Calls

By SAI PRAMESH

As students, we sometimes joke about how many questions our parents ask. Did you eat? How are your studies? When are you coming home? The questions can feel endless.

## The Care Behind Familiar Questions

Imagine those conversations becoming less frequent as time changes things. Questions that once seemed repetitive might become the ones we miss.

It can be easy to overlook care when it is part of an ordinary day.

## Appreciating the Conversations We Have

This is a reminder to value the people who check on us. A familiar question can be worth more than it seems in the moment.

One day, the questions we ignore may become the conversations we wish we could hear again.

## Frequently Asked Questions

### What is the message of this reflection?

Ordinary questions about meals, studies and coming home can be expressions of care worth appreciating while those conversations are part of daily life.

### Which everyday questions do parents ask in the reflection?

They ask about waking up, eating, studies and coming home—ordinary checks that can become familiar parts of student life.

### Why might students take these questions for granted?

Their repetition can make them feel routine or irritating, so the care behind them is easy to overlook.

### What does the reflection ask students to imagine?

It asks them to imagine those conversations becoming less frequent and how differently the familiar questions might then feel.

### What response does the reflection encourage now?

It encourages appreciating the people who check in and valuing the conversations while they are part of everyday life.

## Article copy starts below

# My NIAT Robotics Workshop: Learning a Robotic Arm Through Simulation

By sathwick varma

I did not arrive at the NIAT robotics workshop with high expectations. I had sat through sessions that promised practical learning but mostly delivered slides. This one changed how I thought about the connection between machines and software.

## Starting With a Robotic Arm

The instructor began with the basics: what a robotic arm is, how its parts and joints connect, and where it can be used, including factories and medical settings.

I found myself asking “what if?” questions. The subject started to feel like something I wanted to explore rather than information to memorize.

## Learning Through Simulation

There were no physical robots in the room. We used a computer simulation, which initially made me wonder whether the experience would feel like a game.

Watching the virtual arm respond changed that. I could pause, adjust the code and see what happened. The link between my logic and the arm’s movement became visible.

For me, this was a useful starting point. I could focus on code and control without also troubleshooting a physical setup. It made the logic easier to follow and gave me a clearer basis for approaching hardware later.

## Three Things I Took Away

### Simulation Can Support Real Learning

The experience changed my view of simulation as merely a substitute. Being able to test and revise an idea on screen was useful in its own right.

### Control Systems Became Easier to Picture

Ideas that had felt abstract became connected to something I could observe. I could see how a change in code affected movement.

### I Wanted to Explore Robotics Further

Before the session, robotics felt difficult to approach. Afterwards, I wanted to build something because I had a starting point I understood.

## Why the Session Stayed With Me

What mattered was the understanding I left with. The workshop gave me a way to experiment, make corrections and see the results. That made the next step into robotics feel possible.

## Frequently Asked Questions

### Did we use a physical robot?

No. We worked with a computer simulation of a robotic arm, testing movement and changing code on screen.

### Why did simulation help me?

I could pause, adjust the code and see the result, which helped me connect programming logic with the arm’s movement.

### What did I want to do afterwards?

The session made robotics feel more approachable and left me wanting to build something robotic myself.

### What did the workshop explain before moving into simulation?

It introduced what a robotic arm does, how its parts and joints connect, and examples of where such arms are used.

### How did the session change my understanding of control systems?

Seeing code affect the virtual arm’s movement gave me a concrete way to picture ideas that had previously felt abstract.

# Rushikonda Beach: An NSRIT Student’s Break From Assignments

By K goutham sai

Assignments, records, internals, labs—and the familiar “Bro, did you complete the submission?” College can leave students ready for a change of scene. For me, Rushikonda Beach is part of that lighter side of NSRIT student life.

## Photos, Games and Time by the Water

The sand, sea breeze and beach-road views create a very different atmosphere from a day of submissions. Students become photographers and reel creators, sometimes taking enough pictures to fill a gallery.

Some play cricket or football on the sand. Others sit and talk. The changing colours of the evening sky add another reason to pause.

## The Journey Has Its Own Stories

The outing can become memorable before anyone reaches the beach. Friends sing, make jokes and turn the beach-road stretch into their own travel vlog. Someone usually announces, “Bro, this feels like Goa!”

The original account includes public-bus suggestions, but the connections need checking before they can serve as directions. Confirm the current journey with APSRTC rather than relying on an old route description.

## Snacks and the Student Budget

Corn, ice creams, spicy snacks and cool drinks become part of the trip. Plans to save money can quickly change once snacks and extra auto rides enter the picture.

I prefer the evening atmosphere, when the day feels cooler. Mornings can be peaceful too; the afternoon heat is less appealing.

## A Break I Associate With Friends

Rushikonda is memorable to me because of the company, jokes and time away from assignments. A weekend outing or a small celebration does not need to be elaborate to become part of the college story.

## Frequently Asked Questions

### What do students enjoy in this account of Rushikonda?

The article describes photos, beach games, snacks and time talking with friends away from assignments.

### When does the author prefer visiting?

The author favours evenings for cooler conditions and the changing colours of the sky, while also describing mornings as peaceful.

### Are the bus connections in the original verified?

No. Confirm the boarding points and current onward service with APSRTC before planning the journey.

### What food does the Rushikonda account mention?

It mentions corn, ice creams, spicy snacks and cool drinks as part of the outing, without giving prices or recommending a particular stall.

### Why did the journey become part of the memory?

Friends’ songs, jokes and beach-road views made travelling together feel like part of the outing rather than simply the route to it.

## Article copy starts below

# Simhachalam Temple in Visakhapatnam: History, Traditions and Surroundings

By K goutham sai

Simhachalam Temple, on the hills near Visakhapatnam, brings together religious tradition, carved architecture and green surroundings. This student-written introduction looks at the temple’s cultural significance rather than recounting a personal visit.

## Varaha Narasimha and Temple Tradition

The temple is associated with Lord Varaha Narasimha, combining the Varaha and Narasimha forms of Vishnu. Traditions around the shrine connect it with Prahlada and the story of Vishnu protecting his devotee.

These accounts belong to the temple’s religious tradition. Devotees approach the shrine with prayers and hopes for their families, health and future.

## A Historic Shrine With Carved Architecture

The temple’s history includes royal patronage and inscriptions associated with different rulers. The precise founding date should not be stated as settled: the Visakhapatnam district government says its exact age is unknown. Its history section describes inscriptions and later contributions to the shrine. [Visakhapatnam district government](https://visakhapatnam.ap.gov.in/religion/)

Carved pillars, stone sculpture, halls, inscriptions and decorative mandapams are part of the architectural interest described in the original article. Together, these elements offer students a way to consider craftsmanship, history and worship in the same place.

## The Sandalwood Covering and Chandanotsavam

The deity is normally covered with sandalwood paste. Its ceremonial removal during Chandanotsavam is a significant annual observance, giving devotees an opportunity to see the form beneath the covering. This tradition is also described by the district government. [Official temple overview](https://visakhapatnam.ap.gov.in/religion/)

Festival dates and visiting arrangements should be checked for the relevant year; this article does not provide a current schedule.

## Hills, Greenery and the Approach to the Temple

The source describes hill scenery, forests and a winding approach road as part of the experience of the surroundings. The setting adds another dimension for visitors interested in both the shrine and its landscape.

It also mentions daily worship, offerings and visitor facilities. Specific ritual access, amenities and travel arrangements need current confirmation, so this introduction does not present them as a verified checklist.

## Why It Interests Students

Simhachalam offers subjects to explore across history, temple architecture, mythology and religious practice. Understanding those traditions and noticing the setting can make a visit meaningful beyond simply reaching a destination. The temple’s value in this account lies in that connection between culture, faith and place.

## Frequently Asked Questions

### Which deity is worshipped at Simhachalam?

The article describes Varaha Narasimha, a form associated with both the Varaha and Narasimha incarnations of Vishnu.

### What is distinctive about the sandalwood tradition?

The deity is normally covered with sandalwood paste; its removal for Chandanotsavam is a significant annual observance.

### Why might students be interested in Simhachalam?

The shrine connects subjects such as history, architecture, mythology and religious practice with a distinctive hill setting.

### What architectural features does the article describe?

It mentions carved pillars, stone sculptures, temple halls, inscriptions and decorative mandapams as features of interest.

### What does the article say about the temple’s surroundings?

It describes a hill setting near Visakhapatnam, with greenery and a winding approach road contributing to the setting of the shrine.

## Article copy starts below

# NIAT NSRIT Survival & Food Fest: Stalls, Teamwork and Shared Memories

By Suryarock_69

At NIAT × NSRIT, the Survival & Food Fest brings food stalls, teamwork and friendly competition into the same event. The activity starts before the campus fills with visitors: teams need ideas, menus, themes and a plan for running their stalls.

## Planning a Stall Together

Students brainstorm themes, prepare menus and think about how to attract visitors. Presentation matters alongside the food. Each stall gives a team a chance to turn an idea into something other students can experience.

## When the Campus Becomes a Marketplace

On the day, colourful stalls, food aromas and busy crowds change the atmosphere. Students move between spicy snacks, drinks and homemade specialities, trying different dishes and supporting friends.

The bustle makes the event social as well as competitive. Behind each stall is a group sharing responsibilities and trying to keep things moving.

## What the “Survival” Challenge Adds

Teams need to manage resources, respond to unexpected problems and make decisions under pressure. That creates opportunities to practise communication, leadership and the practical side of running a small venture.

Working together matters as much as having an idea. Planning, creativity and perseverance become visible in the way a team handles the day.

## What Remains After the Stalls Close

The event’s memories include the preparation, laughter and shared effort as well as the food. In this account, the Survival & Food Fest is valuable because it gives students a setting to learn through responsibility while enjoying campus life together.

## Frequently Asked Questions

### What did teams do at the Survival & Food Fest?

They planned menus and themes, prepared stalls, attracted visitors and shared the work of running the event.

### What did the “survival” aspect involve?

The account emphasizes managing resources, dealing with unexpected problems and making decisions under pressure.

### How did teams prepare before the Food Fest?

They brainstormed ideas, developed stall themes, planned menus and considered how their presentation would attract visitors.

### What did visitors do during the event?

Students moved between stalls, tried snacks, drinks and homemade specialities, and supported their friends’ efforts.

### What made the Food Fest memorable beyond the food?

The account emphasizes shared preparation, teamwork, laughter and friendly competition as parts of the experience students carried away.

Working together matters as much as having an idea. Planning, creativity and perseverance become visible in the way a team handles the day.

## What Remains After the Stalls Close

The event’s memories include the preparation, laughter and shared effort as well as the food. In this account, the Survival & Food Fest is valuable because it gives students a setting to learn through responsibility while enjoying campus life together.

## Article copy starts below

# Where I Relax Between Classes at NIAT NSRIT

By K goutham sai

When assignments, presentations and deadlines crowd the day at NIAT × NSRIT, I appreciate having somewhere to pause. A few ordinary campus spaces became the places where my friends and I could talk, laugh and make a stressful day feel lighter.

## Tree Benches and Familiar Company

For me, the best relaxing spot inside the campus is definitely the benches under the trees. After long classes and continuous discussions, sitting there feels calm and refreshing. Most students gather there while waiting for buses or during free time between classes.

That place honestly became more than just a sitting area for our batch. It slowly turned into one of the main student hangout spots where conversations never really end. Sometimes we discuss projects, future plans, and technology. Other times we simply joke around and laugh about random classroom moments.

My friends — Surya, Azam, Sawith Varma, Monica, and Vishali — and I spent many evenings there together. Even stressful situations somehow feel lighter while sitting there with friends and talking freely.

## The Waiting Room Near the Washrooms

Another place students often relax in is the waiting room near the washrooms. Even though it sounds like an ordinary place, it somehow became another unofficial student corner during breaks. People sit there casually discussing assignments, presentations, faculty, and everyday college life.

## Cafeteria Breaks

The cafeteria is another important relaxing spot inside campus. No matter how stressful the day becomes, food breaks somehow improve everyone’s mood. Some students go there mainly for snacks, while others go just to escape classroom stress for a while.

One thing I noticed at NIAT is that students rarely sit quietly during breaks. With our group’s energy and humour, even small free periods quickly become full conversations and laughter.

The funniest moments usually happen during stress discussions. Students begin by seriously talking about deadlines and workload, but within minutes somebody makes a random joke and suddenly the entire group starts laughing instead of stressing.

## When a Faculty Member Joined the Conversation

One memorable moment happened when we were sitting near the tree benches discussing how stressful college life was becoming. Unexpectedly, one of our faculty members joined the conversation with us. At first everyone became silent and awkward, but later the conversation became surprisingly comfortable and funny.

That moment honestly made the campus environment feel more natural and friendly.

## Why Those Small Breaks Matter

Looking back now, I feel relaxing spots inside a campus are important not because of the place itself, but because of the people and memories connected to them. Sometimes the best part of college life is not the classroom or events — it is those small moments spent with friends between stressful classes.

And honestly, those small breaks are what make busy college life feel enjoyable and memorable.

## Frequently Asked Questions

### Where do I like to relax between classes?

I describe the benches under the trees, the waiting room near the washrooms and the cafeteria.

### Which break-time memory stands out?

A faculty member joined our conversation about college stress at the tree benches. The initial awkwardness gave way to a comfortable, funny exchange.

### Who did I spend time with at the tree benches?

My account names Surya, Azam, Sawith Varma, Monica and Vishali as the friends I spent many evenings with there.

### What did our break-time conversations cover?

We talked about projects, technology, future plans and classroom moments. Discussions about workload could quickly turn into jokes and laughter.

### Why did these ordinary spaces become important to me?

The people and conversations gave them meaning. A short break with friends could make a demanding college day feel lighter.

# NIAT NSRIT Campus Greenery: My Break From Screens in Sontyam

By Vishali.k

The green spaces around our NIAT NSRIT campus in Sontyam are a welcome change after an eight-hour coding stretch. Looking out at lawns and the mountain backdrop feels very different from staring at a stubborn UI/UX layout problem.

## A Quiet Patch After a Difficult Peer Review

The academic blocks sit alongside garden patches and outdoor seating. After an intense peer-review session left my creative confidence a little bruised, I took my notebook to a shaded grassy patch near the boundary wall.

I wanted fifteen quiet minutes with trees instead of a screen. For me, that short break was a chance to reset.

## The Sprinkler Had Other Plans

You know that peaceful moment when you lie on the grass to look at the clouds? I managed to choose a spot directly in front of a hidden automated sprinkler.

It switched on with a hiss. My exit was considerably less graceful than my arrival.

## Why I Still Value the Green Spaces

Having outdoor space close to the classrooms makes it easier for me to step away from work for a moment. The break does not need to be elaborate—even if this one became unexpectedly eventful.

I would check for damp soil before putting a college bag down, though. An abstract mud stain across your paperwork is not the souvenir you want for an evening lab.

See you on the lawns—or catching me wiping grass clippings off my laptop.

## Frequently Asked Questions

### What green spaces does the account describe?

It mentions lawns, garden patches, outdoor seating and a shaded grassy spot near the boundary wall, with a mountain backdrop.

### What interrupted my quiet break?

A hidden automated lawn sprinkler turned on just as I was trying to relax on the grass.

### What prompted me to take the outdoor break?

An intense peer-review session had left my creative confidence bruised, so I took a notebook to a shaded grassy patch to sit quietly.

### What did I appreciate about greenery near the classrooms?

It gave me somewhere nearby to step away from a screen and reset after demanding coding work.

### What practical reminder came with the lawn experience?

The account jokes about checking for damp soil before putting a bag down, so a relaxing break does not leave paperwork covered in mud.

## Article copy starts below

# Why Potential Needs Practice: A Short Reflection for Students

By SAI PRAMESH

Being told you have talent or potential can feel encouraging. But potential is only a starting point. It still needs practice.

## Turning Ability Into Action

Ideas need work before they become entries in a competition. Dreams need effort before they become career plans. Waiting to feel motivated can leave both unfinished.

Consistent habits give students a way to keep developing, even when enthusiasm varies.

## What Matters Next

The useful question is what you do with the ability you have. Potential is a promise; practice is how you begin to give it substance.

## Frequently Asked Questions

### What is the main message?

Potential needs action. Practising consistently and building useful habits give ability a chance to develop.

### Does being called talented mean the work is finished?

No. The reflection treats talent as a beginning that still needs practice and effort.

### Why are ideas alone not enough for achievement?

An idea needs action before it becomes a completed piece of work or something to enter in a competition.

### What does the reflection suggest instead of waiting for motivation?

It emphasizes building consistent habits so practice can continue even when enthusiasm varies.

### How does the reflection describe the relationship between potential and effort?

Potential describes what might be possible; effort is the work of developing and using that ability.

# Growth Before Attention: A Student’s Reflection on Recognition

By SAI PRAMESH

Many people want attention. Fewer stop to ask why they are receiving it.

Attention can follow talent or success, but it can also follow controversy. Being noticed does not automatically mean creating something valuable.

## Keeping Growth in View

Chasing recognition can pull attention away from the work or values that mattered in the first place. This reflection argues for focusing on growth, useful work and excellence.

## Recognition With Meaning

There is no need to make attention the measure of every effort. What the work contributes—and what you learn while doing it—can matter more than how widely it is noticed.

## Frequently Asked Questions

### Why question the attention you receive?

The article points out that attention can come from achievement, talent or controversy. Its value depends on what it reflects.

### What can attract attention in this reflection?

Success, talent and controversy are all mentioned as reasons someone might be noticed.

### What risk does the author associate with chasing attention?

The author warns that a focus on recognition can pull people away from themselves and what they value.

### What does the reflection suggest focusing on instead?

It argues for growth, excellence and creating value rather than making attention the main goal.

### Does being widely noticed establish that work is valuable?

Not by itself. The reflection distinguishes the visibility of something from what it contributes.

## Article copy starts below

# When a College Friend Starts to Feel Like Family

By SAI PRAMESH

Some friendships begin with an ordinary moment: a seat in class, a project team or a casual conversation. Nothing about that introduction seems especially important at the time.

## Trust Built Through Shared Experiences

Over time, you celebrate together, face setbacks and support each other through difficult periods. Familiarity becomes trust, and the friendship starts to feel deeper.

## When a Friend Feels Like Family

The bond grows through loyalty and time. A person who began as someone you happened to meet can become someone you depend on. That is what makes these friendships worth appreciating: the steady care that turns an ordinary introduction into a lasting connection.

## Frequently Asked Questions

### What makes a friendship feel like family?

The reflection points to loyalty, trust, shared experiences and support built over time.

### How can the friendship begin in this reflection?

It can start with a classroom seat, a project team or a casual conversation that seems ordinary at the time.

### Which shared experiences deepen the friendship?

Celebrating victories, facing challenges and supporting each other through difficult periods gradually strengthen the bond.

### Why does time matter to the relationship described?

Time allows repeated experiences and dependable support to build trust beyond the initial introduction.

### What does “family” mean in this context?

It describes a sense of closeness and reliance built through friendship, loyalty and care rather than a biological relationship.

## Article copy starts below

# The Friend Who Helps You Improve With Honest Feedback

By SAI PRAMESH

Compliments are easy to enjoy. Honest correction can be harder to hear, especially when it concerns a project, presentation or decision you care about.

## The Feedback That Helps You Improve

A friend who notices a mistake may tell you something uncomfortable. Later, you might recognize that their honesty helped you see what needed work.

Correction is useful when it comes with care and an intention to help.

## Appreciating More Than Praise

Encouragement matters, and so does feedback you can learn from. A friendship can make room for both: someone who celebrates your effort and is honest when there is a way to improve.

## Frequently Asked Questions

### Why can a friend’s correction matter?

Honest feedback about a project, presentation or decision can reveal something you missed and help you improve, even when it feels uncomfortable at first.

### What kinds of mistakes might the friend point out?

The reflection mentions mistakes in projects, presentations or decisions.

### Why can honest feedback feel uncomfortable at first?

It identifies something that needs work in an effort or choice you care about, rather than offering the praise you might have hoped for.

### When does correction become useful in the reflection?

It is useful when it helps someone recognize a mistake and improve, with care behind the honesty.

### Can a supportive friendship include both praise and correction?

Yes. The revised reflection values encouragement alongside feedback that helps a person learn.

# The Friend Who Stayed Humble After Success

By SAI PRAMESH

Success can bring new opportunities, surroundings and social circles. Yet one of the most admirable responses is staying grounded through those changes.

## The Friend Who Remains Familiar

This is the friend who still laughs at the same jokes, treats people with respect and does not let achievement replace their values.

## What Humility Preserves

Achievements may change a lifestyle without needing to change someone’s character. The reflection is about that kind of humility: continuing to treat people well while moving forward.

## Frequently Asked Questions

### What is admirable about the friend in this reflection?

They remain respectful, grounded and familiar even as success changes their opportunities and surroundings.

### What changes can success bring in the reflection?

It can bring new opportunities, environments and circles of people.

### How does the friend show that they remain grounded?

They still laugh at familiar jokes and continue treating people with respect.

### What does the author believe should remain consistent after achievement?

The reflection emphasizes keeping values and character consistent even when a person’s lifestyle changes.

### Why does the author connect humility with respect?

The reflection values how someone treats others alongside their accomplishments; staying humble helps preserve that personal connection.

## Article copy starts below

# NIAT NSRIT Survival & Food Fest: Stalls, Teamwork and Shared Memories

By Suryarock_69

At NIAT × NSRIT, the Survival & Food Fest brings food stalls, teamwork and friendly competition into the same event. The activity starts before the campus fills with visitors: teams need ideas, menus, themes and a plan for running their stalls.

## Planning a Stall Together

Students brainstorm themes, prepare menus and think about how to attract visitors. Presentation matters alongside the food. Each stall gives a team a chance to turn an idea into something other students can experience.

## When the Campus Becomes a Marketplace

On the day, colourful stalls, food aromas and busy crowds change the atmosphere. Students move between spicy snacks, drinks and homemade specialities, trying different dishes and supporting friends.

The bustle makes the event social as well as competitive. Behind each stall is a group sharing responsibilities and trying to keep things moving.

## What the “Survival” Challenge Adds

Teams need to manage resources, respond to unexpected problems and make decisions under pressure. That creates opportunities to practise communication, leadership and the practical side of running a small venture.

Working together matters as much as having an idea. Planning, creativity and perseverance become visible in the way a team handles the day.

## What Remains After the Stalls Close

The event’s memories include the preparation, laughter and shared effort as well as the food. In this account, the Survival & Food Fest is valuable because it gives students a setting to learn through responsibility while enjoying campus life together.

## Frequently Asked Questions

### What did teams do at the Survival & Food Fest?

They planned menus and themes, prepared stalls, attracted visitors and shared the work of running the event.

### What did the “survival” aspect involve?

The account emphasizes managing resources, dealing with unexpected problems and making decisions under pressure.

### How did teams prepare before the Food Fest?

They brainstormed ideas, developed stall themes, planned menus and considered how their presentation would attract visitors.

### What did visitors do during the event?

Students moved between stalls, tried snacks, drinks and homemade specialities, and supported their friends’ efforts.

### What made the Food Fest memorable beyond the food?

The account emphasizes shared preparation, teamwork, laughter and friendly competition as parts of the experience students carried away.

By Suryarock_69

At NIAT × NSRIT, the Survival & Food Fest brings food stalls, teamwork and friendly competition into the same event. The activity starts before the campus fills with visitors: teams need ideas, menus, themes and a plan for running their stalls.

Students brainstorm themes, prepare menus and think about how to attract visitors. Presentation matters alongside the food. Each stall gives a team a chance to turn an idea into something other students can experience.

On the day, colourful stalls, food aromas and busy crowds change the atmosphere. Students move between spicy snacks, drinks and homemade specialities, trying different dishes and supporting friends.

The bustle makes the event social as well as competitive. Behind each stall is a group sharing responsibilities and trying to keep things moving.

## What the “Survival” Challenge Adds

Teams need to manage resources, respond to unexpected problems and make decisions under pressure. That creates opportunities to practise communication, leadership and the practical side of running a small venture.

Working together matters as much as having an idea. Planning, creativity and perseverance become visible in the way a team handles the day.

## What Remains After the Stalls Close

The event’s memories include the preparation, laughter and shared effort as well as the food. In this account, the Survival & Food Fest is valuable because it gives students a setting to learn through responsibility while enjoying campus life together.

## Article copy starts below

# A Positive Learning Environment at NIAT: Why Support Mattered to Me

By Vishali.k

During my first year at NIAT, I realized that effort is only one part of learning. The people around me—and how comfortable I felt asking questions or making mistakes—mattered too.

## Encouragement Made Participation Easier

A word of support could make it easier to try something unfamiliar. Feeling encouraged helped me see questions and uncertainty as part of learning rather than reasons to stay quiet.

Curiosity mattered in that environment. A simple question could lead to a discussion, a project idea or a new interest.

## Learning Together in the Gen AI Club

The Gen AI Club brought students together to discuss artificial intelligence, technology and innovation. What stood out to me was the opportunity to learn from one another.

Sharing knowledge, helping with difficulties and respecting different ideas made participation feel more collaborative. The atmosphere gave creativity and experimentation room to develop.

## A Different Response to Failure

The BRAVE Competition reinforced this lesson. In the experience described here, nearly 15 unsuccessful business negotiations came before a successful 16th attempt.

Those setbacks could have been a reason to stop. Instead, they became opportunities to improve communication, negotiation and resilience. I connected that experience with the value of an environment where mistakes can be discussed and learned from.

## Support in Everyday Interactions

I noticed the importance of small actions: listening to an idea, offering encouragement or helping someone keep going. Trust and friendships grew through those interactions.

Feeling that a contribution would be respected made it easier to speak up, take responsibility and consider unfamiliar ideas. Support did not remove the work; it helped make trying feel worthwhile.

## What I Value About the Environment

Facilities and programmes matter, but so do the people involved in everyday learning. When I look back, I remember encouragement, shared questions and the confidence that developed gradually. For me, those experiences explain why a positive learning environment is such an important part of college.

## Frequently Asked Questions

### What did a positive learning environment mean to me?

It meant feeling able to ask questions, share ideas, make mistakes and learn alongside people who encouraged improvement.

### How does the Gen AI Club fit into the story?

I describe it as a space for discussing AI, sharing knowledge and learning together rather than treating progress as an individual competition.

### How did encouragement affect participation in my account?

Support made asking questions, presenting ideas and trying unfamiliar tasks feel more approachable.

### What example of learning from setbacks does the account give?

It describes nearly 15 unsuccessful BRAVE business negotiations before success on the 16th attempt, with learning in communication and resilience along the way.

### Why do small supportive actions matter in the reflection?

Listening, offering encouragement and respecting an idea can help build trust and make another student more willing to contribute.

# Hostel Life at NIAT: Why the People Mattered More Than the Room

By K goutham sai

Before coming to NIAT, I thought a lot about hostel rooms, amenities and routines. After spending time here, I found that the people I shared daily life with mattered most to me.

## From Strangers to Friends

One of the most interesting things about moving into a hostel is how quickly relationships develop. On the first day, everyone is a stranger. You don't know the people living next door. You don't know your roommate very well. You don't know who you'll spend most of your time with. A few weeks later, everything changes. Those same people become part of your daily life. And often, they become some of your closest friends.

## Learning Independence

For many students, hostel life is their first experience living away from home. At first, that independence feels exciting. Then it feels challenging. Eventually, it becomes empowering. Students learn how to manage their schedules, handle responsibilities, and make decisions on their own. These experiences teach valuable life skills that extend far beyond college.

## Learning to Live With Other People

Some of the lessons that stayed with me came from everyday hostel life. They're learned through everyday experiences. Living with different people. Resolving misunderstandings. Sharing responsibilities. Supporting one another during difficult times. The hostel becomes an unofficial classroom where students learn patience, communication, and adaptability.

## Late-Night Conversations Are Different

Ask any hostel student about their favorite memories, and chances are they'll mention late-night conversations. There's something unique about those moments. The day is over. Classes are finished. Everyone is relaxed. Conversations become more honest, more meaningful, and sometimes much more entertaining. Many friendships become stronger during these simple conversations.

## A Community That Understands You

One of the biggest advantages of hostel life is being surrounded by people who are experiencing similar challenges. Everyone is dealing with assignments. Everyone is preparing for exams. Everyone is trying to balance responsibilities. That shared experience creates a strong sense of understanding and support. Students don't have to explain everything because the people around them already understand.

## The Little Moments Become the Big Memories

When people think about college memories, they often focus on major events. But hostel life teaches a different lesson. Many of the best memories come from ordinary moments. Sharing snacks during a study session. Laughing over an inside joke. Helping a friend prepare for an exam. Celebrating a birthday in the corridor. These moments may seem small, but they become unforgettable over time.

## More Than Accommodation

The hostel isn't simply a place where students stay. It's a place where they grow. They become more independent. They build friendships. They develop confidence. They learn how to live and work with people from different backgrounds. These experiences shape students in ways that academics alone cannot.

## The People I Will Remember

For me, hostel life became a community as well as a place to stay. The conversations, shared responsibilities and small celebrations helped make it feel familiar. Those are the parts of the experience I expect to remember most.

## Frequently Asked Questions

### What mattered most to me about hostel life?

The people: friendships, shared responsibilities, late-night conversations and support during college challenges.

### What does hostel life teach in this account?

The article highlights managing a routine, communicating with different people, resolving misunderstandings and becoming more independent.

### How did relationships change after moving into the hostel?

People who were initially strangers gradually became part of the daily routine, and some developed into close friends.

### Which small hostel memories does the account mention?

It mentions sharing snacks during study, helping with exam preparation, inside jokes and celebrating birthdays in the corridor.

### What made late-night conversations different in this account?

With the day’s classes finished, the atmosphere felt more relaxed and conversations could become more open and personal.

## Article copy starts below

# NIAT NSRIT Student Life: Stress, Friendship and My Starlight 2025 Moment

By K goutham sai

One thing I understood during my first few months at NIAT is that college life here is never only about studies or only about fun. It honestly feels like stress, growth, pressure, excitement, and enjoyment are all happening together at the same time.

## Balancing NSRIT Classes and NIAT Sessions

Almost every day at NIAT feels busy in some way. There are regular NSRIT classes, NIAT technology sessions, assignments, presentations, projects, and activities happening continuously. In the beginning, managing everything felt very difficult because the workload was much higher than I expected.

Sometimes I used to wonder how people were handling both regular academics and industry-focused learning together. There were days when assignments and deadlines piled up together, making the entire week stressful. But slowly, I realized everyone around me was facing the same pressure.

## The Friends Who Made Busy Days Lighter

What made things easier was the atmosphere and the people around me. My friend circle — Surya, Azam, Sawith Varma, Monica, and Vishali — became one of the biggest reasons college life stayed enjoyable even during stressful times. Sometimes one random joke during class was enough to reduce the tension of an entire day.

The funniest thing about college life is that even stressful moments somehow become memories later. Group discussions before submissions, confusion during presentations, and last-minute preparation sessions slowly became part of daily life.

## Finding My Voice at Starlight 2025

At the same time, I could also clearly see my personal growth happening. Earlier, I used to hesitate while speaking publicly or participating in activities. But after joining NIAT, things slowly started changing because students here are constantly encouraged to interact, present, participate, and improve themselves.

One major moment that boosted my confidence was anchoring during our fresher’s event “Starlight 2025.” Even though there was a lot of backstage mess and pressure during the event, standing on stage and speaking confidently in front of everyone felt like a completely different experience for me.

## Conversations About Technology and the Future

Another thing that makes NIAT unique is the student mindset. Many of the students around me were active, tech-focused and interested in improving. Even normal conversations often turn into discussions about coding, projects, AI, startups, internships, or future plans. Those conversations helped keep me motivated to improve.

## The Pressure Was Real Too

Of course, the pressure here is real too. Balancing traditional academic systems with modern industry learning sometimes feels exhausting. There are moments when students feel mentally tired because of the continuous workload.

For me, working through those demands was also part of growing, although it could be exhausting. Looking back now, I can clearly see how much my confidence, communication, technical knowledge, and mindset have changed within just one year.

## Looking Back on the Change

For me, NIAT life feels like a mixture of chaos and growth happening together every single day. It is stressful, fast, competitive, fun, and unforgettable at the same time — and that is exactly what makes the journey special.

## Frequently Asked Questions

### What made my workload challenging?

I was balancing regular NSRIT classes, NIAT technology sessions, assignments, presentations, projects and activities.

### Which experience helped my confidence?

Anchoring the fresher’s event Starlight 2025 stood out, even with pressure and backstage confusion.

### What helped during stressful periods?

My friends—Surya, Azam, Sawith Varma, Monica and Vishali—made busy days easier through conversation and humour.

### What did my everyday conversations with classmates often involve?

Coding, projects, AI, startups, internships and future plans came up often. Those conversations helped keep me interested in developing my skills.

### Did personal growth mean the workload stopped feeling difficult?

No. I still describe periods of exhaustion and mental tiredness alongside growing confidence, communication skills and technical understanding.

## Article copy starts below

# My First Month at NIAT: Lost Classrooms, Lab Friends and a First Presentation

By sathwick varma

I'm going to be honest with you — my first month at NIAT was nothing like I imagined it would be. It was messier, louder, more overwhelming, and somehow also more energizing than anything I had mentally prepared for. Here's what those thirty days actually looked like.

## Week One: Finding Rooms and Questioning My Choice

The first week felt like someone handed me a puzzle with all the pieces but no picture on the box. Orientation sessions, unfamiliar faces everywhere, a schedule that looked like a corporate spreadsheet — it hit all at once and there was no easing into it.

Here's the thing nobody warns you about though: everyone around you is equally lost. That shared confusion becomes the first thing that connects you to people. Our batch WhatsApp group went from zero to 247 unread messages within 48 hours. Roughly half of them were "guys where is the lab?" and the other half were "what's the dress code tomorrow?" Nobody knew. Everyone was figuring it out together.

That collective cluelessness is actually where the first friendships form. Not in any planned icebreaker activity — in the chaos of week one, trying to locate a classroom before the session starts.

*Practical note: save the campus map on your phone before day one. I cannot stress this enough.*

## Week Two: A Familiar Lab Desk and a Faster Pace

By the second week something shifted. Not dramatically, but enough to notice. I knew which chai stall opened earliest. I had silently claimed a desk in the lab that was now understood to be mine. I had maybe three friends who had genuinely stuck around past the polite introductory phase.

The coursework also picked up pace noticeably. The trainers made it clear early that this wasn't going to be a gradual build — NIAT moves at a real speed and the expectation is that you move with it.

What actually surprised me in week two was the energy in the rooms. People here are building things, not just reading about them. There's a difference between a place that talks about practical learning and a place where you walk into a lab and everyone around you is genuinely working on something. NIAT is the second kind. That was the week I stopped wondering if I'd made the right choice.

## Weeks Three and Four: Peer Learning and My First Presentation

By week three I had completely stopped tracking which day of the month it was. Not because I was comfortable — because I was genuinely too occupied to notice.

Projects, peer learning sessions, the kind of late-night "bhai ek cheez samjha" calls that become their own category of social interaction — it had become a whole lifestyle rather than just an academic schedule. I gave my first internal presentation during this stretch, completely lost the thread in the opening thirty seconds, somehow recovered, and walked out with a response that was better than I deserved. That's probably the most honest description of early growth I can offer.

What genuinely caught me off guard was how much my batchmates pushed me forward. Not in a competitive way — in a genuinely collaborative one. I learned more from late-night study sessions in those two weeks than I could have figured out alone in a month. NIAT provides the structure and the content. For me, my people provided the momentum that carried me through.

The person sitting next to you in the lab has probably already spent two hours on the exact problem you just started googling. Just ask. Everyone here is figuring things out and most people are happy to figure them out together.

## What I Think About the First Month Now

Completely. Without hesitation.

It was messy and intense and occasionally overwhelming in ways I didn't anticipate. There were moments in week one where I genuinely questioned whether I was cut out for the pace. There were late nights in week three where I was running on chai and stubbornness and not much else.

But those thirty days were also some of the most genuinely energizing I've had. I began finding my rhythm and the people who stayed beyond the first introductions—the ones I could call at 11 PM when something was not making sense. That gave me a reason to keep going.

Just give it the thirty days. The puzzle starts making sense — even without the box.

## Frequently Asked Questions

### What was most confusing in my first week?

Orientation, unfamiliar faces, finding rooms and understanding the schedule arrived together. Our batch chat reached 247 unread messages within 48 hours.

### What helped by weeks three and four?

Peer learning, late-night study calls and asking classmates for help gave me momentum, including during my first internal presentation.

### What signs of a routine appeared in week two?

I knew which chai stall opened earliest, had a familiar lab desk and had begun forming a small group of friends beyond initial introductions.

### What happened during my first internal presentation?

I lost my thread in the opening thirty seconds, then recovered. The response afterwards was better than I had expected.

### What practical lesson would I pass on from the first month?

Save a campus map before day one and ask classmates when you are stuck. Finding rooms and working through problems became easier with help.
